=== SOURCE CODE (java) ===
package org.example;

import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Random;

public class Snake {
    private int length;
    final int SCREEN_WIDTH;
    final int SCREEN_HEIGHT;
    final int BLOCK_SIZE;
    private List<Position> positions;
    private int score;
    private Position foodPosition;
    private Random random;

    public Snake(int SCREEN_WIDTH, int SCREEN_HEIGHT, int BLOCK_SIZE, Position foodPosition) {
        this.length = 1;
        this.SCREEN_WIDTH = SCREEN_WIDTH;
        this.SCREEN_HEIGHT = SCREEN_HEIGHT;
        this.BLOCK_SIZE = BLOCK_SIZE;
        this.positions = new ArrayList<>();
        this.positions.add(new Position(SCREEN_WIDTH / 2, SCREEN_HEIGHT / 2));
        this.score = 0;
        this.foodPosition = foodPosition;
        this.random = new Random();
    }

    public void move(Position direction) {
        Position cur = positions.get(0);
        int x = direction.getX();
        int y = direction.getY();

        int newX = (cur.getX() + (x * BLOCK_SIZE)) % SCREEN_WIDTH;
        int newY = (cur.getY() + (y * BLOCK_SIZE)) % SCREEN_HEIGHT;

        Position newPosition = new Position(newX, newY);

        if (newPosition.equals(foodPosition)) {
            eatFood();
        }

        if (positions.size() > 2 && positions.subList(2, positions.size()).contains(newPosition)) {
            reset();
        } else {
            positions.add(0, newPosition);
            if (positions.size() > length) {
                positions.remove(positions.size() - 1);
            }
        }
    }

    public void randomFoodPosition() {
        do {
            int x = random.nextInt(SCREEN_WIDTH / BLOCK_SIZE) * BLOCK_SIZE;
            int y = random.nextInt(SCREEN_HEIGHT / BLOCK_SIZE) * BLOCK_SIZE;
            foodPosition = new Position(x, y);
        } while (positions.contains(foodPosition));
    }

    public void reset() {
        length = 1;
        positions.clear();
        positions.add(new Position(SCREEN_WIDTH / 2, SCREEN_HEIGHT / 2));
        score = 0;
        randomFoodPosition();
    }

    public void eatFood() {
        length++;
        score += 100;
        randomFoodPosition();
    }

    public int getLength() {
        return length;
    }

    public List<Position> getPositions() {
        return positions;
    }

    public int getScore() {
        return score;
    }

    public Position getFoodPosition() {
        return foodPosition;
    }

    public static class Position {
        private final int x;
        private final int y;

        public Position(int x, int y) {
            this.x = x;
            this.y = y;
        }

        public int getX() {
            return x;
        }

        public int getY() {
            return y;
        }

        @Override
        public boolean equals(Object o) {
            if (this == o) return true;
            if (o == null || getClass() != o.getClass()) return false;
            Position position = (Position) o;
            return x == position.x && y == position.y;
        }

        @Override
        public int hashCode() {
            return 31 * x + y;
        }
    }
}

=== PROMPT ===
You are an expert programmer. Translate the following java code to py.
- Keep behavior identical, including inputs/outputs, side effects, edge cases, and exceptions.
- Use idiomatic py only when it does not change behavior.

```java
package org.example;

import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Random;

public class Snake {
    private int length;
    final int SCREEN_WIDTH;
    final int SCREEN_HEIGHT;
    final int BLOCK_SIZE;
    private List<Position> positions;
    private int score;
    private Position foodPosition;
    private Random random;

    public Snake(int SCREEN_WIDTH, int SCREEN_HEIGHT, int BLOCK_SIZE, Position foodPosition) {
        this.length = 1;
        this.SCREEN_WIDTH = SCREEN_WIDTH;
        this.SCREEN_HEIGHT = SCREEN_HEIGHT;
        this.BLOCK_SIZE = BLOCK_SIZE;
        this.positions = new ArrayList<>();
        this.positions.add(new Position(SCREEN_WIDTH / 2, SCREEN_HEIGHT / 2));
        this.score = 0;
        this.foodPosition = foodPosition;
        this.random = new Random();
    }

    public void move(Position direction) {
        Position cur = positions.get(0);
        int x = direction.getX();
        int y = direction.getY();

        int newX = (cur.getX() + (x * BLOCK_SIZE)) % SCREEN_WIDTH;
        int newY = (cur.getY() + (y * BLOCK_SIZE)) % SCREEN_HEIGHT;

        Position newPosition = new Position(newX, newY);

        if (newPosition.equals(foodPosition)) {
            eatFood();
        }

        if (positions.size() > 2 && positions.subList(2, positions.size()).contains(newPosition)) {
            reset();
        } else {
            positions.add(0, newPosition);
            if (positions.size() > length) {
                positions.remove(positions.size() - 1);
            }
        }
    }

    public void randomFoodPosition() {
        do {
            int x = random.nextInt(SCREEN_WIDTH / BLOCK_SIZE) * BLOCK_SIZE;
            int y = random.nextInt(SCREEN_HEIGHT / BLOCK_SIZE) * BLOCK_SIZE;
            foodPosition = new Position(x, y);
        } while (positions.contains(foodPosition));
    }

    public void reset() {
        length = 1;
        positions.clear();
        positions.add(new Position(SCREEN_WIDTH / 2, SCREEN_HEIGHT / 2));
        score = 0;
        randomFoodPosition();
    }

    public void eatFood() {
        length++;
        score += 100;
        randomFoodPosition();
    }

    public int getLength() {
        return length;
    }

    public List<Position> getPositions() {
        return positions;
    }

    public int getScore() {
        return score;
    }

    public Position getFoodPosition() {
        return foodPosition;
    }

    public static class Position {
        private final int x;
        private final int y;

        public Position(int x, int y) {
            this.x = x;
            this.y = y;
        }

        public int getX() {
            return x;
        }

        public int getY() {
            return y;
        }

        @Override
        public boolean equals(Object o) {
            if (this == o) return true;
            if (o == null || getClass() != o.getClass()) return false;
            Position position = (Position) o;
            return x == position.x && y == position.y;
        }

        @Override
        public int hashCode() {
            return 31 * x + y;
        }
    }
}
```

Use a short stable mapping path that preserves behavior.

Keep the reasoning compact and single-pass:
- Identify only the pieces that must remain equivalent: signatures, I/O contract, stateful behavior, control flow, and key API semantics.
- Turn that mapping into target-language structure promptly.
- Use one short trap scan for the single most likely language-pair failure mode.
- If a mismatch appears, patch only the affected local section and recheck once.
- If no concrete mismatch appears, close instead of reopening the mapping repeatedly.

Output only the translated py code in a code block.

In Java, `%` is remainder, sign of dividend. Python `%` is modulo, sign of divisor. For positive divisor, Java remainder can be negative, Python modulo non-negative. How to implement Java `%` in Python:
```
def java_mod(a, b):
    r = a % b  # Python modulo
    if r != 0 and (a < 0) != (b < 0):
        r -= b
    return r
```
But b is positive (SCREEN_WIDTH, SCREEN_HEIGHT > 0). So simpler:
```
def java_remainder(a, b):
    r = abs(a) % b
    if a < 0:
        r = -r
    return r
```

```
def java_mod(a, b):
    r = a % b
    if r != 0 and a < 0:
        r -= b
    return r
```
Let's test: a=-10, b=100. Python a%b = 90. r !=0 and a<0 -> r = 90-100 = -10. Good. a=-100, b=100. Python a%b = 0. r==0 -> return 0. Java -100 % 100 = 0. Good. a=10, b=100 -> 10. Good. So use this.

```
def _java_mod(a, b):
    r = a % b
    if r != 0 and a < 0:
        r -= b
    return r
```
But if b is negative? Not. If b is 0, Python raises ZeroDivisionError. Java would throw ArithmeticException. We'll ignore.

Wait, Java's `%` for negative dividend and positive divisor: e.g., -10 % 100 = -10. Our function: a=-10, b=100. Python a%b = 90. r !=0 and a<0 -> r = 90-100 = -10. Good. For a=-100, b=100: Python a%b = 0, r==0 -> 0. Good. For a=10, b=100: 10. Good.

Need to consider if `a` is negative and `r` is 0? Already handled.

Now, let's think about the "stable mapping path" and "single-pass" instruction. We need to output only translated py code in a code block. No extra explanation. We'll produce code.

Let's draft code:

```python
import random


def _java_mod(a, b):
    r = a % b
    if r != 0 and a < 0:
        r -= b
    return r


class Snake:
    class Position:
        def __init__(self, x, y):
            self._x = x
            self._y = y

        def getX(self):
            return self._x

        def getY(self):
            return self._y

        def __eq__(self, other):
            if self is other:
                return True
            if other is None or type(self) is not type(other):
                return False
            return self._x == other._x and self._y == other._y

        def equals(self, other):
            return self.__eq__(other)

        def hashCode(self):
            return 31 * self._x + self._y

        __hash__ = hashCode

    def __init__(self, SCREEN_WIDTH, SCREEN_HEIGHT, BLOCK_SIZE, foodPosition):
        self._SCREEN_WIDTH = SCREEN_WIDTH
        self._SCREEN_HEIGHT = SCREEN_HEIGHT
        self._BLOCK_SIZE = BLOCK_SIZE
        self._length = 1
        self._positions = []
        self._positions.append(Snake.Position(SCREEN_WIDTH // 2, SCREEN_HEIGHT // 2))
        self._score = 0
        self._foodPosition = foodPosition
        self._random = random.Random()

    @property
    def SCREEN_WIDTH(self):
        return self._SCREEN_WIDTH

    @property
    def SCREEN_HEIGHT(self):
        return self._SCREEN_HEIGHT

    @property
    def BLOCK_SIZE(self):
        return self._BLOCK_SIZE

    def move(self, direction):
        cur = self._positions[0]
        x = direction.getX()
        y = direction.getY()

        newX = _java_mod(cur.getX() + (x * self.BLOCK_SIZE), self.SCREEN_WIDTH)
        newY = _java_mod(cur.getY() + (y * self.BLOCK_SIZE), self.SCREEN_HEIGHT)

        newPosition = Snake.Position(newX, newY)

        if newPosition == self._foodPosition:
            self.eatFood()

        if len(self._positions) > 2 and newPosition in self._positions[2:]:
            self.reset()
        else:
            self._positions.insert(0, newPosition)
            if len(self._positions) > self._length:
                self._positions.pop()

    def randomFoodPosition(self):
        while True:
            x = self._random.randrange(self.SCREEN_WIDTH // self.BLOCK_SIZE) * self.BLOCK_SIZE
            y = self._random.randrange(self.SCREEN_HEIGHT // self.BLOCK_SIZE) * self.BLOCK_SIZE
            self._foodPosition = Snake.Position(x, y)
            if self._foodPosition not in self._positions:
                break

    def reset(self):
        self._length = 1
        self._positions.clear()
        self._positions.append(Snake.Position(self.SCREEN_WIDTH // 2, self.SCREEN_HEIGHT // 2))
        self._score = 0
        self.randomFoodPosition()

    def eatFood(self):
        self._length += 1
        self._score += 100
        self.randomFoodPosition()

    def getLength(self):
        return self._length

    def getPositions(self):
        return self._positions

    def getScore(self):
        return self._score

    def getFoodPosition(self):
        return self._foodPosition
```

We can use `collections.namedtuple` or `dataclass(frozen=True)` to get immutability, but then getX/getY methods? We can define a class with `__slots__` and properties. Let's consider.

If we want Position to be immutable like Java, we can define:
```
class Position:
    __slots__ = ('_x', '_y')
    def __init__(self, x, y):
        self._x = x
        self._y = y
    @property
    def x(self):
        return self._x
    @property
    def y(self):
        return self._y
    def getX(self):
        return self._x
    def getY(self):
        return self._y
    ...
```
But external can still assign `pos._x = 10`. To prevent, override `__setattr__`:
```
def __setattr__(self, name, value):
    if hasattr(self, name):
        raise AttributeError("Position is immutable")
    super().__setattr__(name, value)
```

Let's write final code:

```python
import random


def _java_mod(a, b):
    r = a % b
    if r != 0 and a < 0:
        r -= b
    return r


class Snake:
    class Position:
        __slots__ = ('_x', '_y')

        def __init__(self, x, y):
            self._x = x
            self._y = y

        def __setattr__(self, name, value):
            if hasattr(self, name):
                raise AttributeError("Position is immutable")
            super().__setattr__(name, value)

        def getX(self):
            return self._x

        def getY(self):
            return self._y

        def __eq__(self, other):
            if self is other:
                return True
            if other is None or type(self) is not type(other):
                return False
            return self._x == other._x and self._y == other._y

        def equals(self, other):
            return self.__eq__(other)

        def hashCode(self):
            return 31 * self._x + self._y

        __hash__ = hashCode

    def __init__(self, SCREEN_WIDTH, SCREEN_HEIGHT, BLOCK_SIZE, foodPosition):
        self._SCREEN_WIDTH = SCREEN_WIDTH
        self._SCREEN_HEIGHT = SCREEN_HEIGHT
        self._BLOCK_SIZE = BLOCK_SIZE
        self._length = 1
        self._positions = []
        self._positions.append(Snake.Position(SCREEN_WIDTH // 2, SCREEN_HEIGHT // 2))
        self._score = 0
        self._foodPosition = foodPosition
        self._random = random.Random()

    @property
    def SCREEN_WIDTH(self):
        return self._SCREEN_WIDTH

    @property
    def SCREEN_HEIGHT(self):
        return self._SCREEN_HEIGHT

    @property
    def BLOCK_SIZE(self):
        return self._BLOCK_SIZE

    def move(self, direction):
        cur = self._positions[0]
        x = direction.getX()
        y = direction.getY()

        newX = _java_mod(cur.getX() + (x * self.BLOCK_SIZE), self.SCREEN_WIDTH)
        newY = _java_mod(cur.getY() + (y * self.BLOCK_SIZE), self.SCREEN_HEIGHT)

        newPosition = Snake.Position(newX, newY)

        if newPosition == self._foodPosition:
            self.eatFood()

        if len(self._positions) > 2 and newPosition in self._positions[2:]:
            self.reset()
        else:
            self._positions.insert(0, newPosition)
            if len(self._positions) > self._length:
                self._positions.pop()

    def randomFoodPosition(self):
        while True:
            x = self._random.randrange(self.SCREEN_WIDTH // self.BLOCK_SIZE) * self.BLOCK_SIZE
            y = self._random.randrange(self.SCREEN_HEIGHT // self.BLOCK_SIZE) * self.BLOCK_SIZE
            self._foodPosition = Snake.Position(x, y)
            if self._foodPosition not in self._positions:
                break

    def reset(self):
        self._length = 1
        self._positions.clear()
        self._positions.append(Snake.Position(self.SCREEN_WIDTH // 2, self.SCREEN_HEIGHT // 2))
        self._score = 0
        self.randomFoodPosition()

    def eatFood(self):
        self._length += 1
        self._score += 100
        self.randomFoodPosition()

    def getLength(self):
        return self._length

    def getPositions(self):
        return self._positions

    def getScore(self):
        return self._score

    def getFoodPosition(self):
        return self._foodPosition
```

- `__init__` calls `self._x = 1`. `__setattr__('_x', 1)`. `hasattr(self, '_x')` -> `getattr(self, '_x')` raises AttributeError because not set yet -> returns False. `super().__setattr__('_x', 1)` sets slot. Good.
- Then `self._y = 2`. `hasattr(self, '_y')` -> False. Sets. Good.
- If later `p._x = 3`, `hasattr(self, '_x')` -> True, raises AttributeError. Good.

But wait: `hasattr(self, '_x')` after `_x` is set returns True. But `hasattr` calls `getattr`, which for `_x` returns 1. Good.

Potential issue: `__setattr__` is called during unpickling? Not relevant.

Potential issue: `__hash__ = hashCode` is a class attribute. No conflict.

Let's run a mental test:

```
s = Snake(100, 100, 10, Snake.Position(50, 50))
s.getLength() -> 1
s.getPositions() -> [Position(50,50)]
s.move(Snake.Position(1,0))
cur = Position(50,50), x=1, y=0, newX = _java_mod(50+10,100)=60, newY=50.
newPosition=Position(60,50). Not food. len(positions)=1 not >2. insert -> [Position(60,50), Position(50,50)]. len=2 > length=1 -> pop -> [Position(60,50)]. Good.
```

Another test with collision:
```
s = Snake(100,100,10, food)
s._positions = [Position(50,50), Position(40,50), Position(30,50)]  # length 3? Actually length should be 3, but _length maybe 1. Let's set _length=3.
s.move(Position(-1,0)) -> newPosition = Position(40,50) (neck). len(positions)=3 >2 and newPosition in positions[2:] -> positions[2:] = [Position(30,50)]; not contains. So insert -> [Position(40,50), Position(50,50), Position(40,50), Position(30,50)]; len=4 > length=3 -> pop -> [Position(40,50), Position(50,50), Position(40,50)]. Duplicate. Java same.
```

Another test with collision with tail:
```
positions = [head(50,50), neck(40,50), tail(30,50)] length=3. Move direction (-1,0) from head? Actually newPosition = (40,50) neck, not collision. Move direction (-2,0)? newPosition = (30,50) tail. positions[2:] = [tail(30,50)] contains -> reset. Good.
```

Another test with food:
```
food at (60,50). Move (1,0). newPosition = (60,50) equals food -> eatFood -> length=2, score=100, randomFoodPosition. Then collision check: len(positions)=1 not >2. insert newPosition -> [ (60,50), (50,50) ]; len=2 > length=2? 2 > 2 false, so no pop. Snake length 2. Good.
```

Another test with food and collision? Not possible.

Need to consider if `Position` class's `hashCode` method should be `def hashCode(self):` and `__hash__ = hashCode`. In Python, `__hash__` is a function object. When Python calls `hash(obj)`, it calls `obj.__hash__()`. Since `__hash__` is a function, it's bound to the instance, so it receives `self`. Good.

Need to consider if `Position` class's `__hash__` should return an int. `hashCode` returns int. Good.

Since `_x` is not set, `getattr` raises AttributeError. `hasattr` returns False. Then `super().__setattr__('_x', x)` sets. Good. Then `self._y = y`, `hasattr(self, '_y')` returns False. Sets. Good.

It includes `import random`, `_java_mod`, `Snake` class, `Position` nested class, properties, methods. It seems correct.

However, I need to consider if the model's code is actually correct and if there are any hidden issues. Let's review the code in the response:

```python
import random


def _java_mod(a, b):
    r = a % b
    if r != 0 and a < 0:
        r -= b
    return r


class Snake:
    class Position:
        __slots__ = ('_x', '_y')

        def __init__(self, x, y):
            self._x = x
            self._y = y

        def __setattr__(self, name, value):
            if hasattr(self, name):
                raise AttributeError("Position is immutable")
            super().__setattr__(name, value)

        def getX(self):
            return self._x

        def getY(self):
            return self._y

        def __eq__(self, other):
            if self is other:
                return True
            if other is None or type(self) is not type(other):
                return False
            return self._x == other._x and self._y == other._y

        def equals(self, other):
            return self.__eq__(other)

        def hashCode(self):
            return 31 * self._x + self._y

        __hash__ = hashCode

    def __init__(self, SCREEN_WIDTH, SCREEN_HEIGHT, BLOCK_SIZE, foodPosition):
        self._SCREEN_WIDTH = SCREEN_WIDTH
        self._SCREEN_HEIGHT = SCREEN_HEIGHT
        self._BLOCK_SIZE = BLOCK_SIZE
        self._length = 1
        self._positions = []
        self._positions.append(Snake.Position(SCREEN_WIDTH // 2, SCREEN_HEIGHT // 2))
        self._score = 0
        self._foodPosition = foodPosition
        self._random = random.Random()

    @property
    def SCREEN_WIDTH(self):
        return self._SCREEN_WIDTH

    @property
    def SCREEN_HEIGHT(self):
        return self._SCREEN_HEIGHT

    @property
    def BLOCK_SIZE(self):
        return self._BLOCK_SIZE

    def move(self, direction):
        cur = self._positions[0]
        x = direction.getX()
        y = direction.getY()

        newX = _java_mod(cur.getX() + (x * self.BLOCK_SIZE), self.SCREEN_WIDTH)
        newY = _java_mod(cur.getY() + (y * self.BLOCK_SIZE), self.SCREEN_HEIGHT)

        newPosition = Snake.Position(newX, newY)

        if newPosition == self._foodPosition:
            self.eatFood()

        if len(self._positions) > 2 and newPosition in self._positions[2:]:
            self.reset()
        else:
            self._positions.insert(0, newPosition)
            if len(self._positions) > self._length:
                self._positions.pop()

    def randomFoodPosition(self):
        while True:
            x = self._random.randrange(self.SCREEN_WIDTH // self.BLOCK_SIZE) * self.BLOCK_SIZE
            y = self._random.randrange(self.SCREEN_HEIGHT // self.BLOCK_SIZE) * self.BLOCK_SIZE
            self._foodPosition = Snake.Position(x, y)
            if self._foodPosition not in self._positions:
                break

    def reset(self):
        self._length = 1
        self._positions.clear()
        self._positions.append(Snake.Position(self.SCREEN_WIDTH // 2, self.SCREEN_HEIGHT // 2))
        self._score = 0
        self.randomFoodPosition()

    def eatFood(self):
        self._length += 1
        self._score += 100
        self.randomFoodPosition()

    def getLength(self):
        return self._length

    def getPositions(self):
        return self._positions

    def getScore(self):
        return self._score

    def getFoodPosition(self):
        return self._foodPosition
```

This looks good. It addresses the Java modulo issue with `_java_mod`. It uses `//` for integer division. It uses `Snake.Position` inside methods. It defines `__eq__`, `equals`, `hashCode`, `__hash__`. It uses `__slots__` and `__setattr__` for immutability. It uses properties for final fields.

Potential issues:
- In `Position.__setattr__`, `hasattr(self, name)` for `_x` before set returns False, but for `_y` before set returns False. However, after `_x` is set, `hasattr(self, '_x')` returns True. Good. But what about `__hash__ = hashCode`? That's class attribute, not instance. Good.
- In `Position.__eq__`, `type(self) is not type(other)` matches Java's `getClass() != o.getClass()`. Good.
- In `Position.hashCode`, returns `31 * self._x + self._y`. Good.
- In `Snake.move`, `_java_mod` is used. Good.
- In `Snake.randomFoodPosition`, `randrange` with `SCREEN_WIDTH // BLOCK_SIZE` could raise ValueError if 0, but Java would raise IllegalArgumentException. This is a minor discrepancy. The prompt says "exceptions" but this edge case is unlikely. Could be improved but not necessary.
- In `Snake.randomFoodPosition`, if `SCREEN_WIDTH // BLOCK_SIZE` is 0, `randrange` raises ValueError. If we wanted to match Java exactly, we could define a custom exception, but not needed.
- In `Snake.move`, if `direction` is None, AttributeError vs NullPointerException. Not likely.
- In `Snake.move`, if `newPosition == self._foodPosition` and `_foodPosition` is None, `__eq__` returns False. Good.
- In `Snake.move`, if `len(self._positions) > 2 and newPosition in self._positions[2:]`, slicing creates a copy. Good.
- In `Snake.move`, after `eatFood`, `_length` incremented, then if `len(self._positions) > self._length` pop. Good.
- In `Snake.reset`, `randomFoodPosition` is called. Good.
- In `Snake.eatFood`, `randomFoodPosition` is called. Good.

The code is syntactically correct and behaviorally equivalent for normal use.

That's okay because the user asked "Output only the translated py code in a code block." The model complied.

=== TRANSLATED CODE (py) ===
import random


def _java_mod(a, b):
    r = a % b
    if r != 0 and a < 0:
        r -= b
    return r


class Snake:
    class Position:
        __slots__ = ('_x', '_y')

        def __init__(self, x, y):
            self._x = x
            self._y = y

        def __setattr__(self, name, value):
            if hasattr(self, name):
                raise AttributeError("Position is immutable")
            super().__setattr__(name, value)

        def getX(self):
            return self._x

        def getY(self):
            return self._y

        def __eq__(self, other):
            if self is other:
                return True
            if other is None or type(self) is not type(other):
                return False
            return self._x == other._x and self._y == other._y

        def equals(self, other):
            return self.__eq__(other)

        def hashCode(self):
            return 31 * self._x + self._y

        __hash__ = hashCode

    def __init__(self, SCREEN_WIDTH, SCREEN_HEIGHT, BLOCK_SIZE, foodPosition):
        self._SCREEN_WIDTH = SCREEN_WIDTH
        self._SCREEN_HEIGHT = SCREEN_HEIGHT
        self._BLOCK_SIZE = BLOCK_SIZE
        self._length = 1
        self._positions = []
        self._positions.append(Snake.Position(SCREEN_WIDTH // 2, SCREEN_HEIGHT // 2))
        self._score = 0
        self._foodPosition = foodPosition
        self._random = random.Random()

    @property
    def SCREEN_WIDTH(self):
        return self._SCREEN_WIDTH

    @property
    def SCREEN_HEIGHT(self):
        return self._SCREEN_HEIGHT

    @property
    def BLOCK_SIZE(self):
        return self._BLOCK_SIZE

    def move(self, direction):
        cur = self._positions[0]
        x = direction.getX()
        y = direction.getY()

        newX = _java_mod(cur.getX() + (x * self.BLOCK_SIZE), self.SCREEN_WIDTH)
        newY = _java_mod(cur.getY() + (y * self.BLOCK_SIZE), self.SCREEN_HEIGHT)

        newPosition = Snake.Position(newX, newY)

        if newPosition == self._foodPosition:
            self.eatFood()

        if len(self._positions) > 2 and newPosition in self._positions[2:]:
            self.reset()
        else:
            self._positions.insert(0, newPosition)
            if len(self._positions) > self._length:
                self._positions.pop()

    def randomFoodPosition(self):
        while True:
            x = self._random.randrange(self.SCREEN_WIDTH // self.BLOCK_SIZE) * self.BLOCK_SIZE
            y = self._random.randrange(self.SCREEN_HEIGHT // self.BLOCK_SIZE) * self.BLOCK_SIZE
            self._foodPosition = Snake.Position(x, y)
            if self._foodPosition not in self._positions:
                break

    def reset(self):
        self._length = 1
        self._positions.clear()
        self._positions.append(Snake.Position(self.SCREEN_WIDTH // 2, self.SCREEN_HEIGHT // 2))
        self._score = 0
        self.randomFoodPosition()

    def eatFood(self):
        self._length += 1
        self._score += 100
        self.randomFoodPosition()

    def getLength(self):
        return self._length

    def getPositions(self):
        return self._positions

    def getScore(self):
        return self._score

    def getFoodPosition(self):
        return self._foodPosition
